金利上昇による企業の負担増加は 
いつ本格化するか

○ 日銀の政策金利や、金融市場で決まる国債金利に比べ、企業が実際に負担する金利の上昇はまだ
限定的。企業の新規借入金利や負債残高全体への影響波及に時間がかかることが背景 
○ 金融機関の新規貸出金利は、概ね3カ月かけて政策金利の変化を反映する傾向。特に地域金融機関
で上昇がやや遅く、企業規模により有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが異なる一因に 
○ 借入・社債金利の動きを踏まえると、企業の利払い負担増加は2026～2028年度に本格化し、年間
約3兆円の利益下押し要因に。これまで以上に資本効率を意識した経営が求められる

１．政策金利や国債金利が上昇するも、企業の利払い負担増加はまだ本格化せず 
日本銀行は2026年9月の金融政策決定会合で、 政策金利を1.0％から1.25％へ引き上げた。これは1995
年以来、31年ぶりの高水準である1。また、金融市場で決まる10年国債金利（いわゆる長期金利）は、
2026年9月に節目となる3％を超えた。こちらも1996年以来30年ぶりの高さであり、政策金利や国債金
利はまさに歴史的な転換点を迎えている。 
一方で、 企業が実際に負担する金利の上昇は今のところ限定的だ。 図表1は、 企業の有利子負債利子
率 （＝支払利息等÷有利子負債残高） を指標金利 （政策金利、10年国債金利）と比較したものである。

### Page 2

2

図中の凡例にある数字は、 各金利についてマイナス金利政策解除 （2024年3月19日 ）前 の2023年10～12
月期と、直近の2026年4～6月期を比較した値だ。政策金利が＋0.93％Pt、10年国債金利が＋1.80％Pt
と大きく上昇した一方で、企業の有利子負債利子率は＋0.42％Ptと上昇幅が小さく、企業の利払い負
担の増加はまだ本格化していない。実際、日銀短観における企業の資金繰りに関する調査（図表2）を
みると、「楽である」の回答率から「苦しい」の回答率を差し引いたDIが、2021年以降プラス圏で概
ね横ばいを維持している。企業の実感としても、これまでに実施された複数回の利上げや、金融市場
における国債金利の大幅な上昇は、まだ資金調達環境の悪化につながっていないようだ。 
なぜ、こうしたギャップが生じるのか。主な要因として考えられるのは次の2点である。 
第一の要因は、指標金利と企業の新規調達金利とのタイムラグだ。政策金利などの指標金利が変化
してから、企業が新たに資金を調達する際の金利が変化するまで、少し時間がかかる。特に、企業の
有利子負債の半分強を占める金融機関からの借入において（図表3）、新規調達金利のタイムラグが生
じやすいと考えられる。 
第二の要因は、企業の新規調達金利と有利子負債残高全体の利払い負担とのタイムラグだ。企業が
新たに資金調達する際の金利が上昇しても、それが有利子負債残高全体にすぐ反映されるわけではな
い。 企業の金融機関借入金の4～5割程度が固定金利型であるほか2、 主に大企業が発行する社債の多く
が固定利付債であり、過去の低金利時代に調達した負債がまだ残っているためだ。 
本稿では、 これら2つの要因を踏まえて、政策金利・国債金利などの指標金利上昇を受けた企業の利
払い負担増加が、いつ本格的に現れるかを試算する3。 
２．金融機関からの新規借入金利は、地域金融機関で上昇ペースが遅い傾向 
まず、第一の要因（指標金利から企業の新規調達金利へのタイムラグ）の影響を検証しよう。図表
3に示したように、 企業の有利子負債には金融機関などからの借入金と、 主に大企業が発行する社債が
ある。このうち、社債を新規発行する際の金利は概ね国債金利に連動して決まり、タイムラグがほぼ
図表 3 企業の有利子負債残高の内訳 図表 4 金融機関業態別の新規貸出金利

含まれるが、 シェアが小さい （0.1％程度） ため表示し

### Page 3

3

無いため、ここでの検証は金融機関などから新規に借り入れる際の金利が対象になる。 
図表4は、金融機関の新規貸出金利（金融機関からみた貸出）を、業態別に示したものだ。都市銀行
では新規貸出金利の上昇ペースが速い一方、第二地方銀行や信用金庫では政策金利の上昇に比べて新
規貸出金利の上昇ペースがやや遅い。こうした地域金融機関における金利上昇の遅れが、指標金利と
企業の新規調達金利のタイムラグに影響していると考えられる。 
金融機関の業態によって新規貸出金利の上昇ペースに差があるのは、貸出の金利形態別シェアが異
なることが一因であろう。大手行では企業向け貸出全体の約6割を占める変動金利型貸出のうち、ほと
んどが「市場金利連動」であるため、金融市場の変化による影響がすぐに反映されやすい。他方で、
地方銀行や信用金庫では変動金利型貸出のうち「短期プライムレート連動」のシェアが高い。短期プ
ライムレートとは、各金融機関が設定する企業向け貸出の最優遇金利である。政策金利の変更などを
反映して決められることが多いが、各金融機関の競争環境などによって反映時期・度合いに差が生じ
る4。これにより、第二地方銀行や信用金庫では都市銀行に比べて新規貸出金利の上昇ペースが遅くな
る傾向があると考えられる5。 
こうした金融機関業態別の違いは、企業サイドにおいて、有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが規模別
に異なる一因になっている可能性がある。足元の金利上昇局面では、都市銀行からの借入が多いと考
えられる大企業（資本金10億円以上）で有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが速く、信用金庫など地域金
融機関からの借入が多いと考えられる小規模企業（同1千万円未満）で上昇ペースが遅い（図表5）。
2010年代の金利低下局面では大企業の有利子負債利子率が小規模企業を下回る傾向にあったが、足元
では完全に逆転している。大企業は社債が有利子負債残高の約3分の1を占めるため、金融機関の新規
貸出金利のみが原因とは言えないものの、金融機関業態別の違いが企業規模別の資金調達環境に影響
を及ぼしている可能性がある点は興味深い。 
では、今後日銀がさらに利上げを進めていくと、金融機関の新規貸出金利はどの程度のペースで、
どこまで上昇していくか。過去の政策金利と新規貸出金利の関係を統計的に分析すると、 政策金利1％
図表 5 企業規模別の有利子負債利子率 図表 6 金融機関の新規貸出金利の試算

平均値で除した値、対象は金融・保険を除く全産業

### Page 4

4

Ptの上昇は新規貸出金利を当月に0.31％Pt押し上げ、その後さらに3カ月間かけて当月からの累計で
0.85％Pt押し上げる傾向があることが明らかになった。日銀が今後2027 年半ばにかけて政策金利を
2.0％まで引き上げていくことを前提にすると6、金融機関の新規貸出金利（2026年8月時点で1.78％）
は2027年秋ごろにかけて2.65％程度へ上昇すると試算される （図表6） 。 マイナス金利政策の解除前と
比較すると、2％Pt弱の上昇になる計算だ。 
３．企業の利払い負担増加は今後 2028 年度にかけて本格化。 利益を年間約 3 兆円下押し 
次に、第二の要因（企業の新規調達金利と有利子負債残高全体の利払い負担とのタイムラグ）の影
響を検証しよう。金融機関の新規貸出金利や社債の新規発行金利の上昇が、それぞれの残高全体にど
の程度のペースで反映されるかを確認し、企業の利払い負担がいつ本格化に増加するか試算する。 
金融機関の新規貸出金利と貸出残高全体の金利を比較すると、今回の金利上昇局面では新規貸出金
の上昇に対して貸出金利残高全体の金利上昇がやや遅れている（図表7）。 先に述べたように、金融機
関の貸出の一部が固定金利型であり、新規貸出や借り換えに伴って新規貸出金利の上昇が残高全体へ
波及するのに時間がかかるためだ。 
両者の過去の関係を統計的に分析すると、新規貸出金利の上昇がおよそ5年程度かけて残高全体の金
利を緩やかに押し上げる傾向があるとの結果になった。 図表6で試算したように、 先行きの新規貸出金
利が2027年秋ごろにかけて2.65％程度まで上昇していくと想定すると、貸出残高全体の金利（2026年
8月時点で1.51％ ）は2027年末に2.0％程度、2028年末に2.3％程度と緩やかに上昇し、2030年代前半に
は新規貸出金利と同じ2.65％程度へ収束していく計算になる。 
同様に社債について、 新発債金利と既発債金利を比較したものが図表8だ。 新発債金利は2022年ごろ
から上昇して足元では2％台後半に達しているが、既発債金利はまだ1％強にとどまっており、金融機
関の貸出金利に比べると残高全体への反映に時間がかかりやすいことがうかがえる。なお、日本で新

### Page 5

5

規に発行される社債の平均年限は現在5～6年であり、新発債金利は概ね5 年国債金利に連動して動く
傾向があることから、先に述べたように指標金利→新発債金利のタイムラグはほとんど生じない。 
今後、社債残高が物価上昇にあわせて年間＋2％のペースで増加していくとの前提を置き、 先行きの
償還に伴う借り換えや追加的な新規発行が行われていく場合、既発債金利は2030年代初めにかけて年
間＋0.3％Pt程度の緩やかなペースで上昇していくと試算される（新発債金利は5年国債金利の先行き
予測値に連動すると想定）。7年債や10年債といった年限が長めの社債も一定の規模があることから、
新発債金利の上昇が既発債金利に反映されるまでに時間を要する結果になった。 
最後に、金融機関の貸出金利（企業からみた借入金利7）と社債金利の試算結果を用いて、企業の有
利子負債利回りへの影響を検証する。企業の有利子負債に占める借入と社債の金額シェアを先行き一
定として、 貸出金利と社債金利の変化を有利子負債利子率に反映したものが図表9だ。2023～2025年度
の有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースは年間＋0.1～＋0.2％Pt程度と緩やかだったが、2026～2028年度に
は同＋0.3～＋0.4％Ptに加速すると見込まれる。企業の有利子負債の多くを占める借入金の金利上昇
が、主な押し上げ要因だ。2029年以後は借入金の金利上昇が落ち着く一方、社債金利の上昇継続を受
け、有利子負債利子率は2030年代にかけて緩やかに3％程度の水準まで上昇していくだろう。 
このように、金利上昇による企業の利払い負担増加は今後2028年度にかけて本格化していく見込み
だ。企業全体の有利子負債残高が先行き年間＋2％のペースで拡大していくと仮定すると、 支払利息等
が年間約3兆円増加する計算であり、経常利益（金融・保険を除くベースで2025年度：124.8兆円）に
対し年間2.4％の押し下げ要因に相当する8。 
今後、有利子負債利子率が上昇していく過程では、それに見合った利益率を達成できるかが問われ
るだろう。2025年度時点では投下資本利益率（ROIC、3.70％）の有利子負債利子率（1.45％）に対す
るスプレッドが2％Ptを上回っており、ROICでみた企業の資本効率は外部資金調達コストを十分カバー
できている（図表10）9。しかし、 有利子負債利子率が今後3％程度へ上昇していくと、ROICが現在の水
準のままではスプレッドがコロナ禍初期の2020年度並みに縮小し、企業の利払い負担に対する利益の
図表 9 企業の有利子負債利子率の試算 図表 10 有利子負債利子率と投下資本利益率

（注） 有利子負債利子率の対象は、金融 ・ 保険を除く全産業 ・
全規模。先行きは、法人企業統計調査における借入金
残高と社債残高のシェアを一定として、有利子負債利
子率への影響を試算

6

余力が弱まってしまう。日本の金利上昇が一段と進み、企業の利払い負担が本格的に増加していくと

［参考文献］ 
みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）「2026年・2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ～転換点は近いのか、 投資
主導経済の行方を探る～」、2026年10月2日

1 現在の政策金利は無担保コール翌日物金利の誘導目標だが、1995 年時点では公定歩合を政策金利と見なして時系列比較してお
り、厳密には政策金利の意味するものが異なる。 
2 日本銀行「金融システムレポート」 （2026年4 月）の図表Ⅲ-1-12 によれば、法人向け貸出のうち、大手行で約40％、地域銀
行（地方銀行、第二地方銀行）や信用金庫で 45～50％程度が固定金利貸出である（2025 年 9 月末時点）。 
3 金利上昇による企業への影響には、利払いの増加によるデメリットだけでなく、利子収入の増加によるメリットもある。本稿
では、そのうちデメリットの面だけに焦点を当てて分析しており、メリットについては稿を改めて論じたい。 
4 日本銀行「金融システムレポート」 （2025年4 月）の図表Ⅵ-1-8 では、地域銀行を対象に、2024 年7 月の政策金利上昇を受け
た短期プライムレートの引き上げタイミング、引き上げ幅、引き上げ後金利の貸出先適用割合について分析が行われている。 
5 図表 4 に示した金融機関業態別の新規貸出金利には、企業向け以外の貸出も含まれる。したがって、企業向け貸出における
「市場金利連動」 、 「短期プライムレート連動」のシェアが業態別に異なることだけでなく、貸出全体に占める住宅ローン貸出
のシェアが業態別に異なることなども、新規貸出金利の上昇ペースの違いに影響していると考えられる。 
6 みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）の見通しに基づく想定。 
7 金融機関の貸出金利には企業向け以外の貸出も含まれるが、ここでは簡易的に、企業向け貸出金利が貸出全体の金利に連動す
ると仮定して試算した。 
8 本稿では金利上昇による利払いの増加のみを計算対象としているため、利子収入の増加も考慮すれば経常利益への下押し圧力
は緩和すると考えられる。 
9 投下資本利益率（ROIC）と本来比較すべきは、負債だけでなく自己資本のコストも考慮した加重平均資本コスト（WACC）だ
が、本稿では金利上昇の影響を直接評価する観点から、投下資本利益率と有利子負債利子率を比べている。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

2026 年 10 月 30 日

調査レポート

2026 年 7～9 月期の GDP（1 次速報）予測

～ほぼゼロ成長も、緩やかな景気回復は継続～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田 隼平

2026 年 11 月 16 日に内閣府が公表する 7～9 月期の実質 GDP（1 次速報値）は、前期比＋0.0%（前期比年
率換算＋0.1%）と予測する。設備投資や政府消費の増加に支えられ、かろうじて 4 四半期連続のプラス成長を
維持したものの、個人消費や輸出が前期の一時的な押し上げ要因の剥落もあって減少したことで、ほぼゼロ成長
にとどまったと見込まれる。なお、輸入が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直したことで外需寄与度は大きめのマイナスと
なるものの、公的在庫のプラス寄与により、おおむね相殺されたと考えられる。 
また、名目 GDP は、前期比＋0.9%（年率換算＋3.8%）となったと見込まれる。コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進
む中で、GDP デフレーターは前年比＋2.7%（季節調整済前期比では＋0.9%）と高めの伸びが続いた。 
今回、7～9 月期の実質 GDP はほぼゼロ成長にとどまる見込みだが、イラン情勢の悪化という逆風を踏まえれ
ば、国内景気は底堅さを維持している 。先行きについては、輸入コストの高止まりに伴う価格転嫁の進展が景気
の重しとなる可能性はあるものの、良好な雇用・所得環境や企業業績の改善を背景に内需の回復基調が維持さ
れることなどから、国内景気は緩やかな回復を続けると期待される。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度 
（出所）内閣府「四半期別 GDP 速報」により作成

### Page 2

2 / 2

需要項目別の動向は以下の通りである。

① 実質個人消費（前期比 －0.1%） 
 良好な所得環境が維持され る中、消費者マインドも持ち直しへ向かったものの、前期に 耐久財
が自動車やエアコンを中心に 一時的な要因によって増加した反動 が生じた とみられる 。価格転
嫁の進展による 物価上昇 、地震や台風といった自然災害の発生 も重しとなった 可能性がある 。 
② 実質住宅投資（前期比 －0.3%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数 が横ばいとなる中 、建築コストが上昇していることもあり、 住
宅投資は小幅ながら減少が続いたとみられる 。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比 ＋0.9%） 
 企業の投資意欲は 省力化やデジタル需要 などに支えられて 堅調であり 、設備投資は 3 四半期
ぶりに増加 したと見込まれる 。機械設備投資の供給側統計である資本財総供給（除く輸送機械）
も 7～9 月期は前期比 ＋5.3%と増加した。 
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度 －0.0%ポイント ） 
 前期に 供給制約懸念の高まりを受けて、メーカーが製品在庫などの積み増しを進めた 反動もあ
り、7～9 月期はマイナス 寄与になったと考えられる 。 
 なお、公的在庫 について は、政府による代替調達の進展により 石油備蓄の 減少ペースが大きく鈍
化したことから、前期比寄与度 ＋0.5% ポイントと プラス寄与になったと みられる 。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費支出（前期比＋ 0.3%） 
 高齢化が進む中、医療費 ・介護費を中心に 底堅く推移 したと予測する。  
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比 －2.7%） 
 建築・土木コストの上昇が続いている中、 7～8 月の建設総合統計（出来高ベース：公共） は弱
めの動きとなった ことから 、実質公共投資は 2 四半期 連続で 減少したと見込まれる。  
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度 －0.5%ポイント 、実質輸出：前期比 －0.5%、実質輸入：同 ＋1.7%） 
 輸出は 前期比 －0.5%と減少したと予測する。財は 堅調な 米国向け や中東向けの持ち直しを受
けて増加 した一方、サービスは 前期に 研究開発サービスが一時 的な 要因により上振れた 反動
で減少 したとみられる。  
 輸入 は前期比 ＋1.7%と増加したと 予測する。 特に財が鉱物性燃料 を中心に 持ち直した。ただ
し、サービス については 減少した 可能性がある 。 
 結果 、輸出から輸入を差し引いた 外需 の寄与度は －0.5%ポイント とマイナス になった と見込ま
れる。

### Page 1

Summary: Ardentec released its 3Q26 results and held its investor meeting after market 
hours on 27 October. EPS of TWD3.1 beat our estimate (TWD3.04) and the consensus 
(TWD2.89) by 2.1% and 7.3%, respectively. Operating profit was broadly in line with our 
forecast, but beat consensus by 5%, driven by the Longtan plant ramp-up and higher 
utilisation rates.

We have a Buy (1) rating and 12M TP of TWD395, based on target PER of 25x on our 2027E 
EPS.

What’s the impact:

• Stronger-than-expected 3Q26 gross margin: Ardentec’s 3Q26 revenue reached 
TWD5,337m (+17.7% QoQ, +48.5% YoY), broadly in line with both our estimate and the 
consensus. The strong revenue growth was primarily driven by volume ramp-up at the 
Longtan facilities and supported by spillover demand. Gross margin came in at 45.4% 
(+5.2ppt QoQ, +9.7ppt YoY), beating our forecast and consensus expectations by 0.3ppt 
and 1.7ppt, respectively, mainly due to the Longtan plant ramp-up and higher utilisation 
rates (approximately 80%). Operating income was TWD1,860m (+35.0% QoQ, +112.8% 
YoY), largely in line with our estimate, but beating the consensus by 5.1%. Net profit of 
TWD1,473m, largely in line with both our estimate and the consensus. Although 3Q26 
EPS came in at TWD3.1, beating our estimate and the consensus by 2.1% and 7.3%, 
respectively, the variance was mainly due to share-dilution timing instead of operational 
issues.

• 4Q26 guidance largely in-line: Management guided for 4Q26 revenue to be flat or 
slightly up QoQ on traditional seasonality, which is in line with our forecast (up 3% 
QoQ). On margins, management guided for a slight QoQ decline in gross margin, 
mainly due to higher labour cost and depreciation expenses, which is also consistent 
with our forecast. We estimate 4Q26 gross margin at 44.0%, down 1.4ppt QoQ.

• Solid demand for 2027: For 2027, we expect Ardentec to continue benefiting from the 
AI-driven growth trend, supported by (1) robust wafer sorting outsourcing demand from 
tier-1 foundry customers and (2) strong AI networking demand from a major U.S. 
networking customer. The company is currently engaged in several co-packaged optics 
(CPO) projects with leading U.S. industry players, which could provide it long term 
opportunities and a larger addressable market.

Document 122: 261027_daiwa_TSMT

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

Background: We met with Taiwan Surface Mounting Technology (TSMT) 
on 27 October. According to management, its product mix for 3Q26 was 
TFT-LCD 47%, Mini LED 9%, DRAM module 15%, auto 10%, cable 7%, 
touch 4% and others 8%. TSMT is positive on its optical transceiver, DRAM 
module, auto, low-earth orbit satellites (LEO), server and robotic segments. 
The QFII holding in TSMT was 29.7% on 22 October vs. 19.8% at the end 
of 2025.

Highlights: 4Q26 and 2027 outlook update – an upward revision. 
TSMT guided for its 4Q26 revenue to grow by 5-8% QoQ, and 2027 
revenue to grow by at least 20% (some upside potential subject to raw 
material conditions) for 2027 thanks to multiple revenue drivers, including 
optical transceivers, DRAM module, auto, LEO, server and robotic 
segments. For 4Q26 and 2027, the company expects its quarterly revenue 
and gross margin to further expand sequentially on a more favourable 
product mix from optical transceiver, LEO and server revenue. TSMT 
expects its gross margin to improve to 15-17% in 1H27 and over 20% in 
2H27 on the solid optical transceiver order outlook.

Strong revenue and margin outlook is expected to be driven mainly 
by the optical transceiver segment. TSMT mentioned several revenue 
drivers for 2027-28; among them, optical transceiver surface mounting 
technology (SMT) assembly is the most significant one. TSMT is a major 
global supplier for 800G and 1.6T products. A US-based client has noted 
that the 1.6T delivery schedule was ahead of its previous expectation (from 
4Q26 to 3Q26). The company has thus won an additional US-based tier-
one client (expected to have a larger contribution than the first optical client 
from 3Q27) and a China-based client over the past few months. TSMT is 
also in talks with a new US-based tier-one optical transceiver client. 
According to TSMT, optical transceiver revenue will likely account for 5-
10% of total revenue in 1Q27, 10-15% in 2Q27 and 15-20% in 3Q27; for 
2028, the company aims for 30-40%. Given the pure outsourcing (ie, 
includes no buying and selling of material) nature of the optical transceiver 
business, the segment brings upside potential to the gross margin level, 
per management. Aside from the optical transceiver segment, the company 
also sees strong revenue momentum in 2027-2028 from the DRAM module 
(2027 revenue up 30+% YoY), auto, robotic, LEO and server segments. 
TSMT says it has been expanding its global capacity and adjusting its 
equipment to fit different usages, eg, transforming from TFT-LCD to optical 
transceiver.

Valuation: The stock is currently trading at PERs of 24.8x/13.8x, based on 
Bloomberg’s 2026E/27E EPS forecasts.

27 October 2026

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

研究部對緯穎(6669)維持買進建議，主係：(1) 3Q26營收季增56%，
大幅優於預期；(2) 4Q26在AWS的ASIC氣冷出貨帶動下，預期4Q營
收季增個位數 ；(3) 雖T3水冷機櫃短期遞延， 但預估27年逐月攀升，
公司仍為主要供應商。(4) AMD MI450 於1H27年放量，客戶與平台
更加多元；(5) 新增之Oracle VR200預期的訂單約2.5K，約在27年
貢獻營收。 綜上所述，研究部上修 2026/2027 年營收至1.46兆元
/3.07兆元(原估1.27兆元/2.38兆元)，EPS上修至143.16元/241.16
元，目前評價僅8~9倍，位於低檔，給予12.5倍本益比，目標價3010
元(2027EPS*12.5PER)。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收大幅優於預期 
緯穎9月營收為1,726億元，月增19.6%，年增100%，整體3Q26
營收達4,346億元 ，季增56.2%，年增63%，營收達成率達到
135%(研究部預估)/128%(市場共識)， 大幅優於預期 ， 主係Meta
通用型及AWS T3氣冷出貨旺盛所致。3Q26財報尚未公布，研究
部上修至毛利率8.9%(原估8.1%)， 季減0.4pts， 優於原先預期 ，
主係通用伺服器占比較高， 產品組合較佳 ，對毛利率形成支撐，
預估3Q EPS約45.56元。

2. 4Q營運動能可望延續 
展望4Q26，雖水冷機櫃須遞延至年底至1H27才會逐漸放量 ，但
仍預期T3氣冷機櫃及通用持續出貨，預估4Q26營收約4,666億
元，季增7.4%，年增59.5%。毛利率8.2% (原估7.3%)，季減
0.7pts， 主係高單價ASIC水冷機櫃占比尚低， 預估EPS約45.18
元。整體而言，2H26在ASIC氣冷及通用伺服器帶動下，營運明
顯優於上半年

3. T3短期雜音不改長期趨勢，2027年水冷逐步放量 
根據供應鏈調查：(1) T3水冷機櫃初期產線磨合不順，客戶因
此下修今年出貨規劃。不過，2027年水冷機櫃出貨規劃仍逐季
攀升，在晶片端的水冷比重也提升 至50~70%，單價為氣冷3-4
倍以上，成為2027年營收成長的主要動能。在AWS下一代T4產
品，公司持續參與機櫃設計，有望採用全水冷設計，預期28年
出貨，為中長期成長動能。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

(2)MI450機櫃部分，Meta專案集中在2027年出貨，單價約450
～500萬美元，訂單逐漸明確，預估約可達5～7K櫃。(3) 新增
Oracle VR200訂單，預期約2.5K櫃，約在27年開始貢獻營收。
公司在GPU伺服器的客戶版圖進一步擴大。

4. 目前評價位於低檔，維持買進建議 
綜上所述，考量各客戶訂單逐漸明確， 研究部上修2026/2027
年營收至1.46兆元/3.07兆元(原估1.27兆元/2.38兆元)，2027
年毛利率雖然因高單價水冷機櫃及GPU機櫃比重提升，下滑至
6.5%(原估7.1%)， 但營收規模大幅擴張 ， 獲利仍可望顯著成長 。
股本部分，緯穎配發股票股利，除權基準日為9/8，配股率達
1.9828倍，股本由18.69億元增至56.02億元，EPS因此依比例
稀釋，故2026/2027年稀釋後EPS上修至143.16元/241.16元。
目前評價僅8~9倍，位於低檔，給予12倍本益比，目標價3010
元(2027EPS*12.5PER)。

本 研 究 報 告 所 載 之 投 資 資 訊 ， 僅 提 供 客 戶 做 為 一 般 投 資 參 考 ， 並 非 針 對 特 定 對 象 提
供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資
料 來 源 ， 但 概 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 作 出 任 何
陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非
（且不應 解 釋 為 ）在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 非 依 法 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 人 士 或
公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之
著 作 權 ， 嚴 禁 抄 襲 與 仿 造 。

读懂 8 月工业企业盈利的结构性修复 ，
2026 年9 月28 日

如何看待利润增长放缓下的行业“温差”？
2026 年8 月27 日

企业利润延续两位数增速，下半年分化仍
是主要特征，2026 年7 月27 日

价格与利润率支撑工业企业利润高增，电
子行业贡献超四成，2026 年6 月29 日

工业企业利润延续改善，上下游分化进一
步加剧，2026 年5 月27 日

价格上涨支撑工业企业利润延续改善，原
材料和新动能表现亮眼，2026 年4 月27 日

价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
             ——9 月工业企业利润数据点评

事件：1-9 月工业企业营收同比增长 6.6%，较去年同期提升
4.2 个百分点，与今年 1-8 月持平；1-9 月工业企业利润同比增长
15.5%，较去年同期提升12.3 个百分点、较今年1-8 月下降0.2 个
百分点，其中 9 月当月利润同比增长 14.4%，较去年同期下降 7.2
个百分点，较今年8 月提升10.2 个百分点。 
量价同时改善，营收利润率小幅抬升，共同支撑 9 月利润增速
边际回升。1-9 月工业企业利润同比增长 15.5%，其中 9 月工业企
业利润同比增长 14.4%，较上月回升 10.2 个百分点。营收方面，
1-9 月工业企业营收同比增长6.6%，与1-8 月持平。其中，量的方
面，外需强劲支撑工业生产，9 月出口交货值同比增长 17%，较上
月提升 5.9 个百分点，带动 9 月工业增加值同比增长 5.5%，较上
月提升 0.3 个百分点。价的方面，受中东局势紧张、AI 产业快速
发展、新能源相关需求抬升等因素影响，国际原油、有色等大宗
商品价格上行，带动9 月PPI 同比增长5%，较上月大幅提升1.2 个
百分点。营收利润率方面，1-9 月营业收入利润率为 5.68%，我们
测算 9 月单月营业收入利润率为 5.81%、较上月上升 0.14 个百分
点，结合成本费用来看，9 月单月成本率较上月下降 0.89 个百分
点至84.65%、费用率较上月上升0.4 个百分点至8.35%，整体来看
9 月企业盈利能力较上月稍有改善。
工业企业利润数据点评 2026 年10 月27 日   2026 年第59 期

www.ccxi.com.cn 
9 月工业企业利润数据点评： 
价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
企业产成品库存与上月基本持平，应收账款回收期略有缩短 。9 月工业企业产成品存
货同比增长 11.6%，较上月上升 0.6 个百分点，从金额来看，9 月产成品存款 7.37 万亿，
与上月基本持平，但与9 月制造业PMI 数据背离，9 月制造业PMI 产成品库存分项为47.6%、
较上月下降 0.8 个百分点，可能因为制造业 PMI 产成品库存反映的是产成品数量的变化，
而工业企业产成品存货反映的是金额的变化，二者背离说明产成品存货单位成本的上升，
与近期 PPI 走强表现一致。9 月工业企业应收账款余额同比增长 10%，较 1-8 月上升 1 个
百分点，而应收账款回收期较 8 月下滑 0.6 天至 71.6 天，主要是因为 9 月营业收入环比
增速（7.89%）高于应收账款余额环比增速（0.97%），表明 9 月工业企业现销收入增加，
以及应收账款回收力度加大，与9 月企业活期存款增加相一致。 
国有企业利润增速边际抬升，私营企业利润增速边际下滑。1-9 月国企利润同比增长
13.1%，较 1-8 月上升 2.8 个百分点，原油等大宗商品价格上行或是主要原因；私营企业
利润同比增长 8.7%，较 1-8 月下降 1.7 个百分点，今年整体呈“前高后低”态势；股份
制企业利润同比增长20.8%，较1-8 月小幅下降0.4 个百分点；高基数下外商及港澳台企
业利润同比增长0.5%，较1-8 月下降 1.8 个百分点。

2 
www.ccxi.com.cn 
9 月工业企业利润数据点评： 
价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
行业利润分化加剧， 超六成行业利润负增长， 原油与有色相关产业链和电子产业链
利润持续高增，消费品制造业盈利修复仍需时日。1-9 月采矿业利润同比增长 38.2%，较
1-8 月抬升 3.1 个百分点，利润延续高增。国际原油价格高位运行，以及 AI、新能源相关
产业链对有色金属的需求旺盛是主要原因，1-9 月，石油开采和有色金属采选业利润分别
同比增长 21.3%和 68%。1-9 月制造业利润同比增长 17%，较 1-8 月微降0.4 个百分点，原
材料制造业和高技术制造业表现亮眼，1-9 月，原材料制造业利润同比增长 44.9%，其中，
原油相关的化工、化纤制造行业利润分别同比增长 49.2%和 81.7%，有色金属加工业利润
同比增长 79.7%；高技术制造业利润同比增长 52%，其中电子行业利润同比增长 1.0 倍，
拉动全部规模以上工业企业利润增长 9.6 个百分点。但是，终端消费需求疲弱下，消费制
造业利润持续负增长，1-9 月，汽车、农副食品加工、纺织服装、家具制造等行业利润分
别同比下滑 22.8%、17.3%、15.6%和 51.3%。1-9 月公共事业利润同比下滑 14.3%，主要源
于电力、热力生产和供应业利润同比下滑 17.9%，今年以来跌幅持续扩大，煤炭价格上行
抬升生产成本，电力市场化改革推动电价下降，共同挤压电力行业利润。 
与去年同期相比，中游行业利润占比相对稳定，上游、下游行业利润占比一升一降。
1-9 月，上、中、下游行业利润占比
1
分别为 26.26%、50.8%和 22.94%，与上月相比变化不
大，但与去年同期相比，上游、下游行业利润占比分别上升 5.45 和下降 8.18 个百分点，
反映原材料价格上涨以及终端需求疲弱对企业利润的挤压。中游行业利润占比变化不大，
内部出现一定结构性分化，其中，电子产业利润占比较去年同期上升 7.56 个百分点，化

1
 上中下游行业口径较之前有所调整，同时不再计算公共事业行业利润占比。

9 月工业企业利润数据点评： 
价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
工、化纤制造行业利润占比也有小幅提升，而其余行业如金属 /非金属制造、通用/专用/
运输设备制造等行业利润占比则较去年同期有不同程度下降。 
整体来看，在工业生产加快、价格上行带动下，9 月工业企业利润回升至两位数增速。
展望后续，四季度行业利润 或延续改善趋势，但 分化延续，利润仍将向原油、有色金属
以及 AI 相关产业集中。其一，中东冲突局势未定，近来国际原油价格持续维持在 100 美
元/桶的高位，利好石油开采及其相关的化工化纤行业利润增长，同时，冬季采暖叠加对
原油的替代需求增加，煤炭行业利润也将延续亮眼表现。其二，AI、新能源产业快速发展，
对铜、铝、锂等有色金属的需求旺盛，支撑有色金属矿采和加工行业利润。其三， AI 投
资热度未减，相关产品价格上涨显著，需求的快速增长带动先进存储、电子电路制造等电
子行业利润快速增长，同时，算力与数据中心建设带动光纤制造、光缆制造等行业利润抬
图5：1-9 月采矿业、制造业利润保持高增 图6：1-9 月利润向上游集中

주가 추이

3분기 영업 이 익 은 지상방산 매출 감소와 일 회성 비용 등의 영 향 으로 
기대치를 대폭 하회했습니다. 4분기 실적 회복 과 중 장기 실적 성장 대
비 저평가된 밸 류 에이 션 에 주목합 니 다. 
3Q26 Review: 영업이익 0.5조원으로 컨센서스 대폭 하회 
3분기 매출액은 6.1조원으로 전년대비 6.0% 감소했다. 항공우주 부문
과 연결자회사들 성장에도 불구하고 지상방산 매출이 전년대비 약 0.9
조원 감소한 영향이다. 지상방산 내수와 수출 매출은 각각 0.7조원, 0.6
조원으로 전년대비 28.6%, 50.4% 감소했다. 내수는 대전 사업장 생산 
중단, 수출은 4분기 납기 집중에 기인한다. 항공우주 매출은 군수사업 
물량 증가로 전년대비 9.3% 증가한 0.7조원을 기록했다. 연결 자회사
인 한화오션과 한화시스템 매출은 각각 3.4조원, 0.8조원으로 전년대비 
12.9%, 30.1% 증가했다. 영업이익은 0.5조원으로 전년대비 49.2% 감
소했으며 영업이익률은 7.4%로 6.3%P 하락했다. 한화오션과 한화시스
템의 이익 증가에도 지상방산 이익 감소와 필리조선소 적자 반영이 영
향을 미쳤다. 지상방산은 일회성 비용 약 460억원, 원화 강세, 매출 감
소에 따른 고정비 부담 증가가 복합적으로 작용했다. 세전이익은 한화
오션의 외화환산손실 영향으로 -0.2조원을 기록하며 적자전환했다.  
실적 회복과 저평가 매력에 주목할 시점 
4분기 영업이익은 1.7조원으로 추정하며 시장 기대치를 상회할 전망이
다. 지상방산 수출 인도 물량이 집중되는 영향이다. 2027년 예상 실적 
기준 P/E는 12.5배로 경쟁사 Rheinmetall(22.5배) 대비 낮다. 약 35조
원의 방산 수주잔고를 기반으로 최소 2029년까지 실적 성장이 예상되
는 만큼 저평가 매력이 높다. 신규 수주 시점과 실적에 대한 우려가 상
존하지만 현재 밸류에이션에는 상당 부분 반영된 것으로 판단한다. 미
국 중간선거 등 국방예산 관련 정책 불확실성과 환율 부담이 완화되면 
투자자들의 관심은 4분기 실적과 2027년 신규 수주로 이동할 전망이
다. 연말 주요국의 국방예산 확정은 신규 수주 시점에 대한 불확실성을 
낮추는 계기가 될 수 있다.  
투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 1,300,000원 유지 
글로벌 재무장 수요 확대에 따른 동사의 중장기 성장 전망은 여전히 
유효하다. 투자의견 BUY와 목표주가 1,300,000원을 유지한다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 30 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (채운샘) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
한화에어로스페이스는 당사와 ‘독점규제 및 공정거래에 관한 법률’ 에 따른 계열회사 관계에 있는 법인입니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

한화에어로스페이스 (012450) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY) 을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%) 이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY) 을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 109,000원 
현재주가 (10.29) 56,700원 
상승여력 92.2%

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국

Document 335: Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

September 2026: Marked improvement in
services business confidence
September 2026: Business climate in the
manufacturing sector comfortably in the
expansion zone for the 8th month in a row

to a 3-year high
Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas

Document 350: Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

10
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AN EXPORT-DRIVEN RECOVERY
Growth, which began in the fourth quarter of 2025, gained momentum in the first half of 2026. This momentum should
continue, with expected growth of 1.1% in 2026 and 1.3% in 2027. In the short term, the main driver will still be exports,
particularly to Europe, which will contribute to restructuring industry. The impact of investment plans is expected to

strengthen in 2027, while domestic demand is likely to remain constrained by inflation (2.7% in 2026 and 2.6 % in 2027),
which will weigh on purchasing power. Fiscal stimulus and structural reforms are expected to continue to support growth.
However, the rise in the deficit and debt levels means that the increase in long-term interest rates observed so far is mostly
structural.
A RETURN TO GROWTH DRIVEN BY EXTERNAL TRADE
The first half of 2026 confirms the rebound in growth (0.4% and then

0.3% q/q in Q1 and Q2). This growth is driven by foreign trade (contribu-
tions from net exports of +0.8 pp and +0.2 pp, respectively, with +0.9 pp
on average coming from exports and -0.4 pp from imports), following
two years of contracting exports, particularly those to Europe (see be -
low). The increase in public expenditure ( see our analysis ) reflects
the implementation of investment plans, with the multiplier effect on
growth of these plans expected to increase.

In the second half of the year, growth is expected to remain robust,
pushing the annual average to 1.1% in 2026 (following 0.3% in 2025).
Exports are set to remain the main driver, thanks to continued strong
European demand, which is compensating for domestic demand that
remains sluggish. Growth of private consumption will, in fact, remain
subdued by energy inflation. Business investment is expected to be
driven by dynamic sectors (defence, technology equipment and aeros -

pace), which would limit the impact of rising interest rates.
In 2027, growth (1.3%) would be based more heavily on domestic de -
mand, with greater spillover effects from investment plans and a likely
recovery in household consumption (following a peak in inflation in
Q1 2027). At the same time, external demand is expected to remain
strong, but its contribution to growth would be lower than in 2026.
This is because the stronger growth in domestic demand would stimu-

late imports to a greater extent.
INDUSTRY IS BENEFITING FROM AI INVESTMENT AND INTRA-EUROPEAN
MOMENTUM
The business climate in industry improved significantly at the start of
the second half of the year (the manufacturing PMI has risen by 4 points
since May, reaching a four-year high in August). New export orders have
even reached their highest level since early 2022. These positive signs
put into perspective the risks associated with the summer’s climate

events (heatwaves and the historic drought on the Rhine), which dis -
rupted industrial activity and river transport.
Since early 2025, output in some sectors has rebounded, particularly in
the electronics sector (+6.4 %). Manufacturers of electrical equipment,
as well as IT and electronics firms, are benefiting in particular from
the wave of investment in data centres and defence. If this momentum
continues, it could offset some of the losses recorded in the automotive

and chemicals sectors, which have both seen the sharpest falls in out-
put since late 2017 (-12.8% and -19.4%, respectively).
The sustained increase in military spending on medical technologies
and robotics across Europe is driving a restructuring of the interme -
diate-goods sector. Companies exposed to struggling sectors are gra -
dually turning towards these new markets. However, this reorientation
is not fully offsetting the loss of historical markets.

The rebound in German industry is also being fuelled by European
demand. While exports to China (-12.7% YTD in July) and the United
States (-1.5%) have certainly continued to fall, exports to a number of
European Union countries have risen sharply, particularly in Southern
and Central Europe. This shift is benefiting machinery and transport
equipment (47.6% of goods exports, up 20.5% since January 2025) in
particular. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027, creating a

virtuous cycle that reinforces European growth.
A STAGNANT LABOUR MARKET
The situation in the German labour market has stabilised over the
past several quarters, but it is not yet showing signs of improvement.
The unemployment rate is shifting relatively little (4% in November;
+0.2 pp y/y) and remains below the Eurozone average (6.4%). Falling
employment in the manufacturing sector (-0.6% since Q4 2019) is offset
by an increase in the services sector (+0.9%). Nevertheless, the latest

business climate surveys suggest that the situation may stabilise. The
gradual retraining of the workforce in struggling sectors is helping to
meet some of the needs arising from new investment.
Generally speaking, in the longer term, pension reform should support
the labour supply. As a matter of fact, it will help to increase the num-
ber of workers gradually, notably through the phasing out of early reti-
rement at 63 and the indexing of the retirement age to life expectancy

from 2032 onwards.
GERMANY
Growth and inflation

The bank
for a changing
world
Although the unemployment rate is set to remain low, wage growth
would remain limited, particularly in 2026, as negotiated wages will
offset inflation to a lesser extent than they will in 2027. The planned
increases in the minimum wage (+8% in 2026 and +5% in 2027, to
EUR 14.6 per hour), significant as they are, will have little knock-on

effect on the rest of the wage scale.
PURCHASING POWER CONTINUES TO BE SQUEEZED BY HIGH INFLATION
Wage purchasing power is expected to fall further in 2026, having
already lost 4 pp since 2022, before stabilising in 2027. The reduction
in income tax (by EUR 10 billion, or 0.2% of GDP), which will come into
force next January, is also expected to support the disposable income of
low- and middle-income households.
Inflation has been rising again since the start of the summer and is

expected to remain above 2% throughout our forecast horizon. It has
accelerated since June (+0.5 pp to +2.9% y/yin August) driven by a fur -
ther rise in energy prices (+6.7 pp to +9.4% y/y), while core inflation re-
mains stable at +2.6%. From spring 2027 onwards, the expected easing
in energy prices (particularly gas prices) should bring down inflation.
However, this decline will be limited by the inflationary effects of the
wider roll-out of investment plans, as well as by the expected rise in

food prices.
This inflation, which has been structurally higher since the onset of the
2022 energy shock, will weigh on household consumption. Furthermore,
German households’ propensity to save remains high by international
standards (with a net balance 15 points higher on average over the
last ten years, according to the European Commission), which is also
weighing on their consumption.
FISCAL STIMULUS CONTINUES
After recording a public deficit of -3% of GDP in 2025, we anticipate a

further deterioration in the public balance to -4% in 2026, then -4.2%
in 2027. The primary deficit is projected to reach -2.8% of GDP in 2026
(after -1.9% in 2025) before narrowing slightly to -2.7% in 2027. At the
same time, the interest burden is expected to rise gradually from 1.1%
of GDP in 2025 to 1.5% in 2027. This combination will result in a rapid
rise in the debt-to-GDP ratio, from 62.6% of GDP in 2025 to 66.1% in
2026 and then 69.7% in 2027, before approaching 85% by the end of the

decade.
The yield on the 10-year Bund rose above 3.5% on 14 September, its
highest level since 2007. In addition to the increase in issues linked to
extra-budgetary funds, inflationary pressures are also contributing to
this rise. A gradual easing of inflation next year would make a mode -
rate easing of sovereign yields possible in 2027. However, yields would
remain high, at 3.2% in Q4 2027, suggesting that the bulk of the rise is
structural.
At the same time, the government is continuing to implement its pro -

gramme of structural reforms, including measures to support business
investment, health-insurance reform, pension reform and an overhaul
of the tax system. A number of measures have come into force, par -
ticularly accelerated depreciation for business investment until 2027,
which will be followed by a reduction in corporation tax from 2028.
Administrative simplification is also set to lead to an overhaul of the
tax system from January 2027 1. The health-insurance reform has been

passed, while the pension reform is still under discussion, with expecta-
tions that it will be adopted by the end of the year.
1  The main measure will be a reduction of EUR 10 billion per year in income tax for individuals on low incomes, funded in part by an increase in taxes on high earners. The bill will
be submitted to Parliament in the autumn, before being implemented from 2027.
Article completed on 14 September 2026

Style Exposure
We are incrementally positive on Jentech with multiple catalysts ahead. Near-term,

we believe the company’s 3Q26 earnings could be the first driver with a potential
GM beat, which should be reported in the second week of Nov. Longer-term, we
believe the drivers are from the incremental adoption of the removable lid in some
Nvidia Rubin GPUs in 2027 (in addition to Rubin Ultra), followed by the
meaningful Google TPU v9 spec upgrade potential in 2028 as another multi-fold
content increase story (based on JPMe), along with a structural margin expansion

trend given continued GPU and ASIC upgrades. Thus, we revise up our 2027E and
2028E EPS materially and are now 28% and 18% above the current BBGe
consensus.
• 3Q26 preview: expecting a stronger GM growth; 4Q26 outlook: slightly
better rev growth. We revise up our 3Q26 GM to 50.1% (vs current BBGe
consensus at 48.7%), which implies another quarter of strong GM expansion
(vs 2Q/1Q26 GM at 46.8%/41.8%), driven by better product mix to more AI
server business. Reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$9.5bn was largely in line with

the company’s guidance, and the 30% QoQ growth was mainly driven by the
decent ramp of Rubin GPU. As the Rubin 1-piece lid shipment should remain
at high volume into 4Q26, we believe the company’s revenue momentum will
also continue, with incremental growth from small volume of removable lid
shipment. Hence, we are now modeling Jentech’s 4Q26 revenue to grow 3%
QoQ, with GM further expanding thanks to ongoing mix enhancement.
• Further 2027 upside from incremental Rubin removable lid adoption;

2028 another earnings level-up year driven by meaningful upgrade in
TPU v9.We revise up our 2027E and 2028E revenue estimates materially by
22% and 14%, which are now 24% and 16% above consensus, respectively. For
the 2027 upward revision, it mainly reflects the incremental adoption of
removable lid (3-piece lid) in partial Rubin GPU from the next 1-2 quarter, in
addition to Rubin Ultra, as long as the company can ramp up its yield and
production of removable lid in the next few months with on-schedule capacity

expansion (new Taoyuan Aerotropolis plant) and equipment move-in (e.g. the
in-house screw locking equipment). More encouragingly, we believe 2028 will
see even greater earnings upside with Google TPU v9 likely upgrading its heat
spreader solution significantly to 2-piece or 3-piece lid design, in our view, as
the potential chip size (4-die) and TDP (potentially 2k+W or even higher)
should be a big level-up compared to v8, which makes the current stiffener-

only solution insufficient. As the content of 2-piece or 3-piece lid solution
could be 5-10x higher than the v8 stiffener, Google TPU v9 (with meaningful
volume) could drive the next wave of the earnings upgrade cycle for Jentech
from 2028 (following Nvidia Rubin/Rubin Ultra as the major driver in 2027).
• Implications. We lift our 2027E and 2028E EPS by 28% and 17% to NT$193
and NT$295, respectively, reflecting the incremental removable lid adoption

Summary Investment Thesis and Valuation
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component
maker worldwide, with leading production capabilities across
heat spreader products (including stiffener and all kinds of lids).
We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-
28, driven by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI

chips and increasing TAM into new solutions (e.g., CPO switch
ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server
sockets for chip customers. We believe Jentech’s technology
leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings
power in the long term.
Valuation
Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which

is largely in line with Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the
AI server boom started.

Investment Thesis, Valuation and Risks
Jentech Precision Industrial Co. (Overweight; Price Target: NT
$8,800.00)
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component maker worldwide, with
leading production capabilities across heat spreader products (including stiffener and all
kinds of lids). We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-28, driven

by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI chips and increasing TAM into new
solutions (e.g. CPO switch ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server sockets for chip customers. We
believe Jentech’s technology leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings power in the long term.
Valuation

Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which is largely in line with
Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the AI server boom started.
Risks to Rating and Price Target
Downside risks to our rating and price target include: 1) Potential delay in mainstream 3-
piece/removable lid design adoption, and 2) market share loss from competition.

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

We expect a 25bp hike on 4 November by the Riksbank,
alongside a reiteration of the tightening bias. Inﬂation was

Muted inﬂation in September, but goods
prices are picking up
Swedish inﬂation was slightly more subdued than expected in
September. CPIF came in at 1.5% year-on-year as expected, but
CPIF excluding energy was just 0.5%, 0.2ppt below the Riksbank’s
forecast. The softness was concentrated in food and services, with
an unusually large drop in car rental prices playing a role.
But the details were not entirely dovish. Goods inﬂation was
stronger and broad-based, with durable goods prices rising 1.1%

month-on-month and annual inﬂation turning positive for the ﬁrst
time in over a year. That ﬁts with earlier signals from producer
prices and suggests higher global price pressures are starting to
feed through.
We remain aligned with the Riksbank’s projections that CPIF
inﬂation should reach 2.5% and CPIF excluding energy 1.0% in
December. Further on, the outlook remains for further acceleration
in core measures, with CPIF excluding energy set to pass 2.0% by

the summer.
Growth proved resilient again in 3Q, with GDP rising 0.4% quarter-
on-quarter and 2.8% YoY.

energy prices remain elevated. Alongside them, the Riksbank

While the September inﬂation print and stronger krona (which we

### Page 4

There’s no denying that the hawkish shift in September was partly
due to the Swedish krona’s weakness. Since that last policy
meeting, the krona has appreciated c.1.4% on a trade-weighted
basis, driven by a drop in EUR/SEK. That, in turn, has been a
consequence of a tightening in two-year swap rate diﬀerentials of
around 20bp, mirroring a stable outlook for Riksbank tightening
while ECB expectations were revised lower on the back of French-
related risk.

EUR/SEK was also trading 1-2% above our short-term fair value
estimates at the end of September. The subsequent rise in euro
idiosyncratic risk has pushed the pair into a modest 0.5-1%
undervaluation.
We don’t think that is enough to justify a dovish Riksbank surprise.
Despite the recent rebound, SEK remains 4% weaker on a trade-
weighted basis since the start of the year. More importantly, the
hawkish Riksbank stance is itself an endogenous driver of recent

SEK strength, with short-term rate diﬀerentials overwhelmingly
dominant for the currency.
A hold or dovish hike would trigger fresh SEK weakness. With the
energy story still negative and contingent euro idiosyncratic risk
potentially abating, SEK could return to late September levels again
without the Riksbank’s support.
Revising our EUR/SEK target lower
Our EUR/SEK year-end target has hovered around 11.10-11.15 for
the past couple of months. The build-up of substantial EUR-speciﬁc

risk makes a lower target an obvious adjustment. Markets are also
not fully pricing a Riksbank hike next week, with 17bp priced at the
time of writing, nor a February follow-up move, with 40bp priced,
which remains our baseline.
Still, EUR/SEK looks a bit too weak relative to fundamentals. That
points to a lingering risk premium and leaves room for upside if
some France-related risk abates into year-end (our baseline). At the
same time, the external backdrop remains challenging for SEK, with

oil prices still very elevated.

Document 411: STABLECOINS_ What next for crypto assets_ (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/STABLECOINS_ What next for crypto assets_ (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-20

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank
Research

### Page 2

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 192026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

### Page 4

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.

CONSUMER/RETAIL PAYMENT: Still on the periphery
Consumer payments: Not yet there
– Retail adoption is still the most distant use case, especially amongst developed-market consumer credit card spend. A recent ABA survey found that only
24% of consumers think that stablecoins could provide meaningful benefits.
– Stablecoins still look more like a debit instrument than a credit product. They do not naturally replicate the consumer valueproposition of credit cards (i.e.

rewards, revolving credit, dispute rights, chargebacks, fraud protection and convenience), so there is limited incentive for developed-market consumers to
use them.
– Limited scope for payments also evident in wallets (see chart below). Very little of the stablecoin supply is being held as balances in consumer wallets.
Still, the number of retail payments is incrementally growing, with cross-border, emerging market use cases strongest
– Retail transaction volume: $2.6bn (2020) to $71bn (2024); $66bn YTD in 2026.*

– Strongest use in: (i) economies with hyperinflation; (ii) poor banking access; or (iii) high remittance fees.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium data.  Shows circulating supply by holder type (Sep 2026). Categories calculated as a percentage of total circulating supply. Note that wallets account for money being held vs
payments (money moving). Business wallets in this case refer to those holding smaller balances with quick turnover (i.e. payment services, merchants, gaming), while institutional wallets hold larger balances and turn them

over quickly, i.e. financial-services firms, treasuries, and wallets. Note: *Retail sized transactions are defined as transactions of $250 or less, according to Visa OnChain Analytics.
The amount of stablecoin supply in consumer wallets stands at ~14%

CASH/LIQUDITY MANAGEMENT: Stablecoins are evolving as a major tokenized
contender
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s Tokenised money and the future of FXand Stablecoins and
agentic commerce: new rails, old frictions.
Stablecoins can help treasury management and settlement
– Stablecoins have a strong use case for settling transactions for FX,

treasury operations and collateral management i.e. intra-day repos.
– This is because stablecoins’ 24/7 settlement can address
operational frictions in cross-border transactions.
Poses significant risk to cross-border B2B transactions for banks
– Compared to traditional banks, stablecoins offer substantial relief
from transaction costs. Our equity analysts estimate total customer
processing fee savings to increase from 0 to ~$70bn in 2035, if
consumers switch from traditional banks to stablecoins.

Stablecoins is not a replacement solution to FX settlement risk
– FX settlement requires two currencies to be exchanged
simultaneously. Currently, stablecoins only serve as an intermediary
between the two currency legs, resulting in a “stablecoin sandwich”.
– Overwhelming concentration of USD-stablecoins also limits
stablecoin ability to support widespread payment-versus payment
settlement across currency pairs.
– Stablecoins also face fragmented interoperability/liquidity issues

and regulatory uncertainty across issuers/networks/jurisdictions.
– For more, read our FX report here.
Should we explain how settlement work as it is very
interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?
Probably more relevant than having the stablecoin
market cap here I would say.
A more integrated post-trade process

INSTITUTIONAL ADOPTION: Corporates, card networks, and banks are integrating
stablecoins
Institutions are racing to capture stablecoin market share
– Card networks: Mastercard expanded its settlement capabilities to include
stablecoins, using USDC and Paxos-issued stablecoins.
– Institutions: Exploring stablecoins via developing in-house permissioned

blockchain experiments and/or exploring issuance of proprietary stablecoins.
– >140 companies and financial institutions helped launch Open USD
(OUSD) in Sep 2026. OUSD reached $799mn of supply in 2 weeks.
Signals shift toward consortium-backed/network-led digital dollar
models.
Are stablecoins posing a threat to traditional payment systems?
– Bullish: Stablecoins to surpass traditional rails by the 2030s (Chainalysis).
– ~17% of stablecoin-linked card volume is occurring across Visa’s

business and commercial card programs. Shows growing adoption of
stablecoins for settlement, treasury management and cross-border
commerce.
– Key concerns for banks: Fee pressure from stablecoin adoption →
bank outflows → higher reliance on wholesale funding → pressure on
net interest margins.
– Base case: However, Deutsche Bank equity analysts see more limited near-
term risk. Visa and Mastercard to remain dominant especially in advanced
markets.

– KYC, AML, sanctions screening, wallet monitoring, fraud controls and
jurisdiction-specific limits remain essential for banks.
Stablecoin transactions could match Visa and Mastercard transactions
sometime between 2031 and 2039 (Chainalysis).
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Chainalysis.
Shows projected years until crypto merchant transactions reach Visa scale.

GEOPOLITICS: Why stablecoins are a "Genius" idea for the US
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, US Treasury, Tether July 2026 report. Shows Major foreign holders of Treasury Securities.
Treasury data from June 2026.
Can sustain USD dominance
– 99% of stablecoins (by market cap) are USD-pegged.
– Stablecoins function primarily as a digital version of dollars

moving over blockchain rails, extending the dollar’s reach into
new digital environments.
– Reinforces dollar savings: The more firms invoice and transact in USD,
the more they save in USD assets. Stablecoins help preserve this
feedback loop by keeping businesses and individuals in dollar
payment rails.
Can support US debt, given share held by foreigners has fallen from
>50% in early 2000s to ~30% in early 2026.
– Stablecoin issuers total holdings: ~$170bn according to Allium x

Citizen.
– Tether: ~$141bn in direct and indirect exposure to US T-bills.
– Impact: BIS shows how $3.5bn inflow into dollar-backed stablecoins
lowers 3m T-bill yields by 2.5-3.5bp under normal conditions. Effect
more than doubles in periods of T-bill shortages.
– Forecasts: T-bills holding by stablecoin issuers could reach ~$1trn by
2028 (Treasury forecasts).
– Recent Fed SF research places it closer to $400bn by the end
of 2030, lower than the US government’s financing needs.

Stablecoin issuers’  demand for US debt could counterweight declining demand from China
Tether competes with Saudi Arabia and South Korea as major holders of US Treasuries
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, US Treasury Data. Shows Treasury
holdings, stablecoin issuers vs China.

US GENIUS ACT: Debate on rules around payment stablecoins one year on
GENIUS provisions
– Strict issuer requirements: Issuers can be private, non-bank entity, but must
meet strict federal or state requirements.
– Reserves: Requires 1:1 backing with US cash, 93 days T-bills, repos/reverse
repos, or money market funds holding those assets.
– AML/KYC/BSA compliance obligations to stablecoin issuers, aligning them

exams, and strong internal controls.
Next steps
– GENIUS takes effect no later than 18 Jan 2027, or 120 days after final
implementing regulations are issued.
– Ongoing pushback from US banks as to whether GENIUS ban on yield-
bearing stablecoins should extend to affiliate or third-party arrangements,
given many users do not hold stablecoins directly with the issuer.
– CLARITY ACT failed in Sep because of disagreements on how
exchanges/broader crypto market structure should handle stablecoins.

– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
There are two ways stablecoin yield can be paid; ongoing debate over the three-
party model
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Congressional Research Service.

RISKS: Still hotly discussed amongst policymakers and financial institutions
Bank liquidity risk
Loss of stable funding source for banks from deposit outflow (not offset by
partial flowback from stablecoin issuers’ reserve deposit)
System stability risk
Stablecoins can fail to maintain their $1 value during times of stress. Evidence shows
that USDC and Tether actually trade within 1% of their dollar value but prices can

diverge sharply during stress episodes.
Run on stablecoin issuer(s) in market stress / trust scenario triggering fire sales of
reserve assets and/or deposit withdrawals.

Markets in Crypto-Asset Regulation
(MiCAR)
Backing Assets*
• 100% backing assets in USD cash or short-term
Treasuries (≤93-day T-bills).
• Non-significant EMTs –At least 30% reserves in
same-currency bank deposits, remainder in HQLA

• Significant EMTs - At least 60% of the reserves in
bank deposits; remainder in HQLA
• Systemic stablecoins – 30% unremunerated BoE
deposits vs 70% in UK sovereign debt (HQLA)
• Non-systemic – Full 1:1 backing in HQLR assets,
minimum 5% in on demand deposits.
UK
Multi-issuance
Stance
• Rules are still being fine-tuned; no specific focus
yet on multi-issuance.
• Multi-issuance under active review; EC considers multi-
issuance permissible, an equivalence-based framework for

EU/third-country arrangements is under legislative
consideration.
• BoE considers multi-issuance unsuitable for systemic
UK use. Preference for single-issuance models backed
by single reserve (incl. Non-GPB denom. stablecoins) .
UK Cryptoasset Regime (FCA)
UK Systemic Stablecoins Regime (BoE)
Eligible Issuers
• Bank subsidiaries, federal nonbank licensees, or
state issuers with federal registration, with
enhanced oversight for >$10bn circulation.

• EMTs: EU-authorised credit institutions or authorised
electronic money institutions. Restrictions apply to
non-EU currency EMTs (exceeding MiCAR
thresholds) as a means of exchange
• FCA-authorised firms for non-systemic stablecoins,
• Systemic issuers to be designated by HM Treasury
and jointly regulated under BoE/FCA oversight
Balance Sheet
Treatment
• Stablecoins treated as reserve-backed digital
payment assets, segregated in bankruptcy-remote

accounts and treated as cash equivalents
• EMTs are fiat-referenced cryptoassets subject to
MiCAR and e-money requirements
• (OCC/FDIC) Mandates prudential capital buffers
tailored to issuer risk profile.
• (Fed) Framework consistent with bank requirements
• EMI issuers: minimum own funds of 2% of EMT
supply; 3% for significant EMTs. Significant issuers
subject to enhanced risk-management requirements
• Systemic: 6 months operating expenses/largest

plausible loss event
• Non-systemic: 1% av. stablecoins in circulation

Marion Laboure is a Managing Director and Macro Strategist at Deutsche Bank in London, and a Lecturer in Economics and Finance at
Harvard University.
She was previously an economist at the IMF, the Central Bank of Luxembourg, Barclays, and the European Commission. She has twice
received the First Prize of the American Society of Actuaries, was named a rising star in finance by Revue Banque, and is recognised

among the 45 standout women in fintech globally.
She holds a Bachelor's degree in Mathematics from the University of Paris Dauphine, a Master of Research in Economics from the

advisory firms Eurasia Group and Greenmantle.
She holds a dual MA/MSc in International History from Columbia University and the London School of Economics, and a BA in History
from Barnard College, Columbia University.

^New 
Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 27) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.
